## Product decisions and target architecture

- Treat D1–D19 in `00-decisions-and-gates.md` as settled. This is a fresh standalone Databricks product; no SaaS accounts, users, projects, credentials, or deployment records carry over.
- The builder backend is TypeScript/Node.js inside Databricks Apps. The existing React UI is retained and integrated into the Databricks package. Generated child apps remain Python/Streamlit.
- Each installation persists new builder state in its own customer-owned Lakebase Autoscaling database bound as an App resource.
- Preserve complete builder parity: all existing components, all eight story visualizations, all workflow actions, AI tools and streaming, custom/prebuilt modules, resource selectors and binding, preview, export/import between standalone installs, create-new and link-existing child-app flows, deployment, recovery, and audit.
- Pin all workspace calls to the hosting workspace. User-triggered operations use the acting user's supported forwarded identity and permissions; the VDAB service principal is not an escalation fallback.
- Marketplace provider approval, publication, commercial terms, and successful second-workspace consumer installation remain external gates.

It has no project routes, durable repository, Lakebase credentials, schema/migration, or ACL. The `pg@8.23.1` runtime and `@types/pg@8.23.1` build dependency were explicitly approved. User direction prohibits Databricks workspace calls. No local PostgreSQL service will be contacted in this phase; repository, token refresh, HTTP, and migration behavior will be tested with injected fakes.

**Exact files/contracts planned:** Add `server/src/db/{lakebase-credentials.ts,lakebase-pool.ts,migrations.ts,project-repository.ts,local-test-project-repository.ts,project-service.ts}` and append-only `server/src/db/migrations/001_initial_schema.sql`; add `server/test/{lakebase-credentials.test.ts,migrations.test.ts,projects.test.ts}`. Update `server/src/{contracts.ts,identity.ts,http-app.ts,main.ts}`, `server/test/http-app.test.ts`, Step 4 plan, `contracts/http-v1.md`, `workspace-binding-map.md`, `research.md`, `databricks-app/README.md`, package smoke, and package script as needed. Implement project create/list/get and creator-managed editor grants with per-project ACL, explicit install-admin recovery allowlist, audit rows, same-workspace scoping, Lakebase app-identity password rotation, and fresh schema initialization. Do not claim database connection/durability evidence.

**Acceptance criteria:** Strict typecheck; unit tests for OAuth/database credential refresh and expiry, parameterized repository queries and workspace/creator/collaborator/admin filtering, transactional/idempotent migration behavior, and sanitized project HTTP routes; HTTP tests exercise create/list/get/cross-user denial and creator-granted collaborator access; packaged localhost smoke exercises create/list/get without Databricks access; `npm run build`, frontend regression, and packaged-process smoke pass; production package includes the SQL migration and only runtime-approved dependencies.

**Risks:** Official documentation exposes the two OAuth exchanges and bound `PG*` variables but does not demonstrate Node + Lakebase binding end to end. `VDAB_INSTALL_ADMIN_IDS` is an explicit installation-managed recovery allowlist, not a claim about Databricks workspace-admin roles. It accepts a label and `expanded` boolean, and its returned multi-element container supports Python `with` notation. The documentation recommends avoiding nested expanders for responsive appearance; this is guidance, not an API prohibition or a new product-scope decision. The target v1 aggregate expresses child order in the parent's ordered `children` array, so generated child order will follow that explicit portable order rather than trusting arbitrary client `layout.y` values. This documentation does not prove Streamlit runtime or Databricks behavior.

## Step 5m starting record — Tabs layout — offline only

**Starting state:** Step 5l Rows, Step 5k Columns, Step 5j Expander, and the nine supported controls are locally verified. **Official API check:** Read the official current [`st.tabs` API reference](https://docs.streamlit.io/develop/api-reference/layout/st.tabs), which reports v1.66.0. `st.tabs` returns context-manager containers, accepts a label string for the optional default pane, and computes all pane contents on every rerun by default. Keep `on_change="ignore"` to match legacy generation; do not claim lazy execution. A default label cannot distinguish a later duplicate tab label from an earlier one, so reject a component only if its selected `defaultTabIndex` resolves ambiguously. Research details and source links are recorded in `research.md`.

**Acceptance criteria:** The editor supports one or more tab labels, editing and adding labels, choosing the default pane, adding multiple supported controls to the active pane, and confirmed removal of a populated pane with its direct controls removed and later `tabIndex` values shifted. Saves preserve stable child keys, labels, default index, and `{tabIndex, y}` positions; reopening shows the correct pane associations and ordering. Service and generator independently validate property allowlists, label lengths, default index, child references, pane index and position, child type, and no nested layouts. **Risks:** Tabs uses a distinct pane index/position mapping rather than fixed one-child slots. A lossy conversion can move controls between panes or reorder them. Duplicate labels cannot always express a non-first default pane through Streamlit's label-based `default` parameter. Streamlit executes every pane by default, so generated work in inactive panes can still run; custom lazy callbacks remain out of scope and must not be implied. Removing tabs is destructive to their child components and needs explicit confirmation. Workspace runtime/child deployment remains unavailable by user direction.

**Precise next action:** Implement the Tabs property editor, active-pane child flow and safe destructive removal, aggregate validation, deterministic Python generation, fixture, smoke, and local regression. Preserve the verified Rows/Columns/Expander behavior and make no Databricks calls.

## Step 5o starting record — Modal page component — offline only

**Official API check:** Read the official current [`st.dialog` API](https://docs.streamlit.io/develop/api-reference/execution-flow/st.dialog), v1.66.0. Dialogs are declared with `@st.dialog`; calling the decorated function renders a dialog and its contents. Current width choices are `small|medium|large`, position is `center|left|right`, dismissal defaults to allowed, and only one dialog function may be called in a script run. `st.sidebar` is unsupported within dialog contents. The API has no height parameter. The old generator's `width="large"` matches a supported choice; legacy nullable pixel width/height settings have no defined runtime mapping and were ignored by legacy generation. This is API documentation only, not runtime/workspace evidence.

## Step 5p starting record — FileUpload and workspace Volume binding — offline only — 2026-10-19

**Verified starting state:** Step 5o Modal is the last locally verified slice. Current checkout inspection confirms the FileUpload registry entry and `databricksVolume` binding schema already exist, but FileUpload is absent from the standalone component editor, save/import property validation, generated preview dispatch, client API/resource discovery, and packaged smoke. Legacy `FileUploadProps` contains only `Label`; its generator supports multiple files and optional volume upload under a selected catalog/schema/volume plus subfolder. The active Node component catalog contains no resource routes beyond Apps discovery. Existing component property validation is already 536 lines and `http-app.ts` is 632 lines, so new behavior must be isolated in focused modules instead of adding substantial logic to those oversized files. Existing Node and PostgreSQL/Codex processes were observed and left untouched. Existing user edits and all untracked transition implementation are preserved.

**Official current API evidence:** Read official Streamlit `st.file_uploader`, Databricks Apps authorization, Workspace API scopes, Unity Catalog Volume API, Files API, and Volume file operation docs. `st.file_uploader(..., accept_multiple_files=True)` returns `UploadedFile` objects and the platform docs warn `UploadedFile.name` is unsanitized. The Files API uploads raw bytes via `PUT /api/2.0/fs/files{file_path}`, supports `/Volumes/<catalog>/<schema>/<volume>/<path>`, the `files` API scope, and overwrite behavior. Apps docs show Streamlit user-token access via `st.context.headers.get('x-forwarded-access-token')` and list `files`, `catalog.catalogs`, and `catalog.schemas[:read]` as supported Apps scopes. The current Volume API docs require the `unity-catalog` API scope for catalog/schema/volume listing, but `unity-catalog` is not in the Apps authorization guide's supported-scope list. This is a documented scope mismatch; the user has prohibited a workspace test, so support for that specific direct API path is **unproven** and must not be represented as verified or worked around by service-principal fallback.

**Exact files/contracts planned:** Add an isolated Node workspace-resource client/service/local-test adapter and typed resource contracts; register them in `main.ts` and expose same-origin `GET /api/v1/workspace/resources/catalogs`, `/schemas?catalog=...`, and `/volumes?catalog=...&schema=...` through the existing HTTP boundary without accepting host/workspace authority. Each Databricks call is pinned to runtime `DATABRICKS_HOST`, uses only the forwarded caller token, paginates until no continuation token, passes `AbortSignal`, and maps 401/403/upstream errors to sanitized problems. Add frontend workspace-resource API/model/query hooks and a FileUpload property editor with label, current-workspace catalog/schema/volume selection, relative subfolder, loading/empty/error/permission states, and no `workspaceConnectionId`. Extend the project component factory/editor to add FileUpload and persist its optional `databricksVolume` binding. Isolate FileUpload property and binding validation in a new module; add an independent preview generator in `server/src/generation/preview-file-upload.ts`, dispatch support, generated fixture `contracts/fixtures/generated/file-upload-app.py`, focused Node/client tests, and packaged smoke. Update `contracts/http-v1.md`, `workspace-binding-map.md`, `feature-parity-matrix.md`, `05-builder-and-ai.md`, `research.md`, `databricks-app/README.md`, and this handoff. No dependency, Lakebase migration, or legacy source change is expected.

**Acceptance criteria:** FileUpload can be added, edited, bound to a Volume in the hosting workspace, saved and reopened without changing stable keys or ordering, and previewed. Save/import and generation independently validate the label, binding discriminator and nested source/resource shape, optional resource fields, and relative destination path; reject authority fields, malformed nested shapes, absolute paths, traversal (`.`/`..`), control characters, unknown properties, and unsupported component structure. Generated source preserves multiple file selection; when a complete Volume binding exists, it uploads exact file bytes with caller authorization and overwrite behavior to the selected Volume/subfolder; without a Volume binding, it retains the legacy in-memory behavior and gives visible status. Uploaded file names must be reduced to a safe basename and URL-encoded, errors must never include tokens/raw upstream bodies, and duplicate destination names must not silently overwrite one another within a batch. Resource discovery differentiates empty results, missing scope/permission, expired sign-in, and upstream failure. Focused tests, Python AST parsing, package smoke/build, whole client/Node/.NET regression, optional disposable local PostgreSQL suite, and diff check must pass before advancing. No Streamlit runtime or Databricks behavior is claimed from source tests.

**Risks:** Apps' supported scope list and the direct Volume API scope requirement do not visibly align; list-volumes may return a consent/scope denial when called through the installed app. A workspace scope spike is not authorized and must remain an external proof gate. The child-app uploader requires its own `files` user scope plus the viewer's `WRITE VOLUME`, `USE CATALOG`, and `USE SCHEMA` privileges; it must not retry as the child-app service principal. `st.file_uploader` defaults to 200 MB/file in current docs and reports browser-controlled MIME metadata, so extension/type hints are not a security boundary. Workspace resource UI must not turn an inaccessible Volume into an empty list. Generated preview is source-only; actual token forwarding, Files API permission enforcement, runtime size behavior, and path semantics remain unverified.

**Precise next action:** Implement the FileUpload slice offline with separate workspace integration, component contract/editor, generator, tests, and package behavior; record any unresolved OAuth scope mapping explicitly. Do not connect to the Databricks CLI, workspace, app, Lakebase, or Marketplace.

## Step 5q starting record — DataWindow with Unity Catalog binding — offline only — 2026-10-19

**Reconstructed state:** Step 5p FileUpload is the last fully verified slice; its client editor, Node current-workspace catalog/schema/Volume routes, preview helper, tests, fixture, packaged smoke, and progress record are present. This resumed inspection confirms the recorded FileUpload local checks and build artifact timestamps from 2026-10-19; no repository `.log`, `.trx`, or JUnit result file was found. The worktree still contains prior uncommitted transition files and unrelated frontend changes. Existing Node/PostgreSQL processes were observed and will be left untouched. DataWindow exists in the legacy registry and in the Node 33-component catalog, but is not in the standalone editor or preview support set. Its exact defaults are `{label:"Data Table", maxRows:0, scrollable:true, placeholder:null, width:"stretch", hideIndex:null, height:"auto"}`; supported bindings are `unityCatalogTable`, `lakebaseProvisionedDatabase`, and `lakebaseAutoscaling`. Legacy source performs `SELECT *` and adds `LIMIT maxRows` only when positive; Unity Catalog uses a SQL helper, and Lakebase uses a separate database helper. The source renders a subheader and `st.dataframe` with width, height, index, column labels, and null placeholder. The current React DataWindow renderer is a schema-only editor canvas, not a runnable generated implementation. No Node SQL statement client, current-workspace table list route, DataWindow editor, validation, generator, or DataWindow fixture exists. The HTTP contract mentions a future tables route using query keys `catalog`/`schema`, while implemented resource routes use `catalogName`/`schemaName`; this slice will normalize the public route to the existing `catalogName`/`schemaName` naming and update the contract.

**Official platform evidence:** The current Databricks Apps authorization guide (last updated 2026-10-09) supports `sql` as an API scope and `catalog.tables` SDK scope with the `:read` modifier, and forwards the viewer's `x-forwarded-access-token` for user-authorized calls. The Unity Catalog Table List REST API is `GET /api/2.1/unity-catalog/tables`, paginated with `max_results`/`page_token`, and documents the direct API scope as `unity-catalog`; therefore the exact direct REST + Apps scope pairing remains unproven and must stay a workspace proof gate. The SQL Statement Execution API accepts `POST /api/2.0/sql/statements` and requires `sql`, a warehouse ID, viewer warehouse access, and table permissions. An installed App can obtain an existing workspace warehouse ID through a configured SQL warehouse resource and an `app.yaml` `valueFrom` environment variable; this resource gives the child App identity access too, so generated user queries must still use the viewer token and must never fall back to the child service principal. Streamlit 1.66 `st.dataframe` supports width `stretch|content|int`, height `auto|content|stretch|int`, `hide_index`, `column_config`, and placeholder. SQL `INLINE` JSON_ARRAY results are bounded to 25 MiB; this implementation needs to surface oversized/error states and keep `maxRows=0` as the legacy no-explicit-limit behavior. Sources: [Apps authorization](https://docs.databricks.com/aws/en/dev-tools/databricks-apps/auth), [Unity Catalog tables API](https://docs.databricks.com/api/uc-tables/v1/table), [SQL Statement Execution API](https://docs.databricks.com/api/statement-execution/v1/statement-execution), [Apps SQL warehouse resource](https://docs.databricks.com/aws/en/dev-tools/databricks-apps/sql-warehouse), [App environment resources](https://docs.databricks.com/aws/en/dev-tools/databricks-apps/environment-variables), [Streamlit `st.dataframe`](https://docs.streamlit.io/develop/api-reference/data/st.dataframe).

Add `server/src/db/project-component-data-window.ts`, `server/src/generation/preview-data-window.ts`, and `preview-data-window-helpers.ts`; update preview properties/dispatch/source/generator, tests and `contracts/fixtures/generated/data-window-app.py`. Add client table resource model/API/query hook, current-workspace DataWindow editor, project editor/factory integration, API/UI round-trip tests, and standalone lint inclusion. Update HTTP, binding, parity, research, Step 5 plan, Databricks App README and this handoff. Preserve the existing three-binding registry contract and imported component JSON. The first complete slice covers editing, saving/reopening, and runnable-source generation for a Unity Catalog table. Lakebase DataWindow bindings remain preserved and explicitly unsupported by this source slice until their user-authorized runtime transport has a separately complete implementation; do not silently remap them to the App identity.

**Acceptance criteria:** An editor can add DataWindow, select a current-workspace catalog/schema/table, retain table column names/types, configure legacy defaults and valid Streamlit width/height/placeholder/hide-index/max-row options, save/reopen, and preview. Request and generator both validate properties and a complete `unityCatalogTable` binding; workspace authority is never client-selected. Table discovery paginates and sanitizes upstream errors. Generated Python emits escaped, quoted Unity Catalog identifiers, a read-only SELECT with an optional bounded row limit, uses only the viewer's forwarded token against the installed workspace and configured warehouse, handles asynchronous Statement API completion and errors without exposing raw bodies/tokens, and renders the full `st.dataframe` contract with column labels. Incomplete binding may save but cannot produce a fabricated query. Add table-route, helper request/poll/result/error, property validation, fixture AST, save/reopen, preview, packaged smoke and full regression checks. No generated runtime or Databricks behavior is claimed from offline tests.

**Risks and unresolved gates:** Table List REST lists direct `unity-catalog` scope while Apps currently documents SDK `catalog.tables:read`; workspace evidence is absent by the user's instruction. Statement Execution is the documented supported user API scope, but requires an existing SQL warehouse resource and `CAN USE` for each viewer; the builder `app.yaml` and child app deployment slice do not yet declare/select that resource. Generated Python can read a `DATABRICKS_SQL_WAREHOUSE_ID` value injected from an existing `sql-warehouse` resource, but deployment/resource selection remains a subsequent slice. Inline results have a 25 MiB cap; no silent service-principal fallback or external-link URL handling is allowed. The two legacy Lakebase bindings require a separately designed supported transport, caller authorization and customer resource mapping; keep their payloads intact and report that parity row as partial until implemented. No new dependency, migration, direct database change, Databricks connection, app deployment, Marketplace test, or legacy retirement is authorized/expected here.

## Step 5r completion — EditableDataWindow with Unity Catalog Delta — locally verified — 2026-10-19

**Starting state and acceptance:** The Step 5r starting record above captured the partial editor/generator and expected Delta-only write behavior. The completed editor now adds/configures the legacy display options, fixed/dynamic row mode, disabled state, current-workspace table, and selected key column; save/reopen preserves the full binding metadata. Save/import and generation independently validate component properties, table format, names, columns and SQL-safe primitive types. Non-Delta/unreported tables are read-only. Complete Delta bindings produce viewer-token SQL reads plus a JSON change set passed as a named SQL parameter to one atomic `MERGE`; cell values are never inserted into SQL text. Before a non-empty save, generated code queries the full table for null/duplicate selected-key values and validates the edited rows too. All generated code remains preview-only.

**Changed paths/contracts:** Added `databricks-app/server/src/db/project-component-editable-data-window.ts` and `server/src/generation/preview-editable-data-window.ts`. Updated `server/src/db/project-components-input.ts`, `project-component-properties.ts`, `project-component-data-window.ts`, `server/src/contracts.ts`, workspace table metadata parsing in `server/src/workspace-resources-client.ts` and `local-test-workspace-resources-client.ts`, and source dispatch in `server/src/generation/preview-component-dispatch.ts`, `preview-component-source.ts`, `preview-data-window-helpers.ts`, and `project-preview-generator.ts`. Added/updated Node tests in `server/test/project-components-service.test.ts`, `project-preview-generator.test.ts`, and `workspace-resources.test.ts`. Added `xorbix-visual-databricks-app-builder.client/src/components/EditableDataWindowPropertiesEditor.tsx` and updated the DataWindow editor, shared table-binding selector, API parser/tests, table model, component factory/editor, project-flow test, and standalone lint target. Updated `databricks-app/scripts/smoke-app.ts` and generated-source fixtures `data-window-app.py` and `editable-data-window-app.py`. Updated the HTTP contract, parity matrix, workspace-binding map, research, Step 5 plan, app README and this handoff. No new package, route, or migration was added.

**Architecture and constraints:** Node validates saved/imported component aggregates and generated source. React queries same-origin current-workspace resource routes, receives table format/column type metadata, and stores selected resource names and column metadata. Generated Python obtains the current viewer's forwarded token, uses the configured workspace SQL warehouse, and never retries as the child App identity. Current official Databricks docs say Delta primary/unique constraints are informational and not enforced; therefore the generated app verifies the chosen key before writing. The aggregate scan and MERGE are separate statements, so a concurrent writer can invalidate the key check, and scanning the full table can be costly. Non-Delta/unreported sources are view-only. Lakebase bindings remain persisted but unsupported by this slice; workflow callbacks remain pending.

Full client `npm test -- --run` passed **55 files / 327 tests**. `npm run test:postgres` passed **1/1** in a disposable local PostgreSQL 17 cluster and stopped it. `dotnet test Xorbix-Visual-Databricks-App-Builder.slnx --no-restore --verbosity quiet` passed **905, skipped 6, failed 0**; existing legacy package vulnerability and version warnings remain.

## Step 5aa starting record — NetworkWebStory — offline only — 2026-10-19

**Starting state:** Step 5z HoneycombStory passed the focused boundary/generator and editor flow checks, packaged Node/client build and smoke, complete local client suite, local PostgreSQL migrations test, fixture AST parse, diff check, and retained .NET regression. NetworkWebStory exists in the 33-definition component catalog, HTTP definition fixture, legacy DTO/service/query builder/self-contained canvas, and React palette/property/preview renderer, but has no standalone Node property validator, generated-app source preview, editor or project-flow test. The user has no disposable Databricks workspace and explicitly said to build without connecting. This slice is local only; no workspace request, deployment, Lakebase runtime test, or SaaS/.NET retirement is authorized or expected.

**Legacy behavior traced:** Required mappings are source entity ID/label, target entity ID/label, relationship type, and relationship weight. Fourteen optional mappings are time, direction, edge value, frequency, edge risk/status, source/target category, source/target value, source/target health, and source/target risk. Dimension defaults use categorical/none; weight and numeric optional roles use continuous/none. Defaults are title `Network Web Story`; force layout; node size by degree; node color by category; edge width by weight; edge color by relationship type; arrows shown; highlight depth 1; minimum weight 0; maximum 300 nodes and 800 edges. Supported editor bounds are nodes 10–500, edges 10–2,000, highlight depth 1–3; layout is force/radial, node size degree/value/none, node color category/health/risk, edge width weight/value/frequency/none, and edge color relationship type/risk/status/none.

The legacy query selects the six required and all mapped optional columns into a relationship-edge row shape, applies an optional maximum-time filter, quotes identifiers, and caps source rows at 1,000. It does not aggregate in SQL. Python coalesces duplicate `(source, target, relationship type)` edges, sums relationship weight/edge value/frequency, captures available status/risk/direction, and derives node metadata from both endpoints. Node and edge lists are capped by configured limits and degree; the summary contains node/edge totals and warnings. Canvas output is 620 px with force/radial layout, node size/color modes, edge width/color modes, arrows, category/type toggle legends, text search, hover details, depth-based selected-node neighborhood highlighting, drag-to-pan, wheel zoom, and double-click reset. Modes are surface, heat_surface, and ridge; interpolation none/bilinear; color height_metric/risk_score/variance/category; camera front/angled/top/free; fallback aggregation sum/avg/max/min. Height scale is 0.1–5 and grid resolution 10–120. Generated Python finite-coerces all numeric outputs, drops missing X/Y, fills missing height with zero, builds event/callout records, computes variance against baseline or target, ranks three peaks and valleys, and safely replaces an escaped JSON payload in a self-contained HTML canvas. The existing deployment validator requires the selected resource key to appear in the target App's genie_space resources. The legacy generated helper uses the Workspace SDK's default identity for chat calls and query-result retrieval; that behavior does not satisfy the approved viewer-permission rule and must be replaced with direct user-authorized Genie calls, without service-principal fallback. **Official platform findings:** The official Genie Spaces API lists visible spaces with a space_id UUID and title. Listing and chat operations use the Genie scope. Chat mode starts and continues a conversation through POST endpoints and returns an asynchronous message status; a separate message endpoint exposes attachment/query results. The Genie conversation guide recommends 1–5 second polling with backoff and stopping after ten minutes for most queries. The Databricks Apps authorization guide documents the genie user scope and forwarded x-forwarded-access-token header. All generated child-app Genie operations must use the acting viewer's token and the hosting workspace; a 401/403 must never retry under the app identity. Official links/findings are recorded in research.md. This is API-document evidence only, not proof that the installed VDAB App is authorized or that workspace/API execution succeeds.

Add a client GenieSpace model/API/query hook and GenieChat type/property editor/project-flow test; wire the shared component factory/editor and standalone lint manifest. No new dependency, database migration, Databricks call, deployment, or legacy deletion is expected.

Save/import and preview independently validate exact property/binding shapes, stable space ID, bounded height, and allowed component structure. Resource discovery uses the trusted configured DATABRICKS_HOST, caller token, explicit Genie scope requirements, pagination, bounded pages/timeouts, and sanitized permission errors; client-supplied workspace hosts and duplicate/invalid pagination are rejected. Generated Python obtains the forwarded viewer token from Streamlit context and uses standard-library HTTPS calls pinned to DATABRICKS_HOST for list/start/send/get/query-result operations. It retains session-local conversation behavior, visible user/assistant messages, safe SQL display and result table, optional border, and bounded history. It polls asynchronous status within a documented maximum, handles cancel/failure/timeout and expired consent/grant with clear guidance, and never logs or exposes tokens, raw response bodies, or temporary result URLs. No denied call falls back to the child app's service principal. Add workspace-client tests, component validation/generator tests, React add/select/configure/save/preview/reopen flow, fixture AST parse, packaged smoke, full Node/client/.NET regressions, local PostgreSQL check, lint/typecheck/build, and diff check before advancing. Long polling can occupy a Streamlit request and must be bounded/cancellable. Genie API attachments can contain external links with temporary credentials; the implementation should render the built-in query-result API response only and never display or log those URLs. ## Step 5af completion — GenieChat — locally verified offline — 2026-10-20

GenieChat now discovers caller-visible Genie spaces through the installed-workspace Node adapter; saves stable space ID/title and chat settings; validates component input on save/import and again during source generation; and previews a Python/Streamlit chat that calls the hosting workspace's Genie Chat API with the child-app viewer's forwarded token. The helper supports start/follow-up/message polling/query-result calls, bounded exponential polling with manual continuation and a ten-minute maximum, bounded attachments/result rows/result bytes/history, safe SQL and result rendering, sanitized permission/runtime errors, and redirect blocking so the viewer token cannot follow an API redirect. Changing the selected space starts a separate session-local conversation state. The offline editor flow covers add/select/configure/save/reopen/preview.

**Platform behavior correction:** The official Genie Chat-mode API documentation has no cancel operation; the documented cancellation endpoint is for Agent mode. This implementation does not call a Chat-mode cancel API. It stops each poll window after 30 seconds, lets the user request another bounded check, stops after ten minutes, and renders a `CANCELLED` state if Databricks returns one. This is the documented API limit and does not establish that a running Genie query can be cancelled from the child app.

## Step 5ag starting record — ModelChat — offline only — 2026-10-20

**Starting state reconstructed from the repository:** Step 5af GenieChat is the last fully locally verified vertical slice. Its source, golden Python fixture, offline editor flow, package smoke, and regression evidence are present and documented above. ModelChat remains in the 33-type registry, is persisted as a generic component, and has legacy editor/canvas/deployment source, but no standalone add/editor flow, strict Node property validator, serving-endpoint discovery route, or generated preview. Existing `http-v1.md` already reserves `GET /api/v1/workspace/resources/serving-endpoints` with `model-serving` and caller grants. There is no such Node handler/client yet. Git is still `main` at `3ef4017` with the existing transition implementation dirty/untracked; preserve it. `git diff --check` passes with the existing line-ending notices. No transition-specific logs or active VDAB/Databricks process were found. Per the user's latest instruction, do not use the local Databricks CLI/auth, browser, workspace, Lakebase, App APIs, install, deploy, or Marketplace.

**Legacy behavior traced:** `ModelChatComponentDto` and `ModelChatComponentService` use an endpoint app-resource name, title `Model Chat`, border false, and total height 600 pixels (the existing UI clamps height to 300–2,000). The legacy generated chat has a scrollable message area, session-local conversation history, user/assistant chat bubbles, and a thinking state. Its app-resource binding and default `WorkspaceClient` run inference as the child App service principal; that is incompatible with D1/D16 for user-triggered inference. Preserve the visible chat and settings, but bind the selected serving endpoint by its stable current-workspace name and use the child viewer's forwarded token. Do not add a serving endpoint as an App resource, grant `CAN_QUERY` to the child service principal, or fall back to it after a viewer denial. ModelChat supports chat-compatible endpoints only; an endpoint that returns a non-chat shape or is not ready must show a safe, actionable message.

**Official Databricks evidence checked 2026-10-20:** `GET /api/2.0/serving-endpoints` returns an `endpoints` collection and documents the `model-serving` scope; the reference does not describe list pagination. The response may include endpoint `state.ready` and `permission_level`. `POST /serving-endpoints/{name}/invocations` accepts chat `messages` with system/user/assistant roles and returns `choices[].message.content`; the API reference labels these chat fields Public Preview and lists `model-serving` and `model-serving-inference`. The Apps authorization guide's currently supported scope list includes `model-serving` (not `model-serving-inference`) and documents forwarding the viewer token; scope configuration is through App authorization settings or CLI `user_api_scopes`, not `app.yaml`. App model-serving resources grant app identity permissions and inject endpoint resource environment values, so they are not suitable for this user-authorized ModelChat path. Official direct references are in `research.md` and were rechecked at [Serving endpoint list](https://docs.databricks.com/api/model-serving/v1/list-inference-endpoints), [Serving endpoint invocation](https://docs.databricks.com/api/model-serving-query/v1/query), [Apps authorization](https://docs.databricks.com/aws/en/dev-tools/databricks-apps/auth), and [App model-serving resources](https://docs.databricks.com/aws/en/dev-tools/databricks-apps/model-serving). Documentation is not a live workspace test.

**Acceptance criteria:** A signed-in project editor discovers the serving endpoints visible to that user in the installed workspace through a same-origin Node route pinned to trusted `DATABRICKS_HOST`. The boundary uses only the caller's forwarded token, enforces timeout and bounded/validated response shape, sorts and deduplicates stable endpoint names, distinguishes an authorized empty list from 401/403/upstream failure, and never accepts host/workspace authority or retries under app identity. The editor can add ModelChat, select an endpoint, edit title/border/300–2,000-pixel height, save, reopen, and preview. Save/import and generation independently validate exact properties, endpoint name syntax, component shape, and bounds. Preview emits complete deterministic Streamlit chat source with session-local bounded history, accessible user/assistant messages and input, endpoint-ready/chat-response checks, and sanitized actionable failure states. Each inference call uses the viewer token from Streamlit context, the installed workspace host, and the documented chat `messages`/`choices` form; tokens and raw upstream response text never appear in errors/logs, redirects cannot carry the bearer token elsewhere, and no denied request falls back to child-App identity. Update source/API/workspace documentation and packaged smoke. Pass focused Node/client tests, strict typechecks/lint, generated Python AST, full Node/client/.NET regressions, disposable local PostgreSQL integration, production build/package, packaged smoke, and diff check before starting 5ah. None of these local checks is evidence of serving endpoint scope consent or runtime behavior in Databricks.

**Risks and external limits:** The API documents chat request/response fields as Public Preview and does not promise that every listed endpoint accepts chat messages. The invocation API lists `model-serving-inference`, while the Apps guide's supported scope list offers `model-serving`; use the supported App scope and clearly record the invocation pairing as unverified pending a real workspace test. Endpoint visibility, `READY` status, and `CAN_QUERY` information do not prove every user's inference grant. Generated Streamlit forwarded-header availability, request limits, response latency, chat model compatibility, and cost are unverified. Workspace testing is unavailable by the user's explicit direction. The SaaS/.NET source remains the parity reference and cannot be deleted during this slice.

## Step 5ah StartDatabricksJob starting-state record — 2026-10-20

**Starting state reconstructed from source:** The preceding SQL warehouse slice is locally verified offline. Contrary to the planned next-action wording above, most of the Job workflow surface already exists: a paginated `/api/v1/workspace/resources/jobs` route backed by `/api/2.2/jobs/list`, caller-token forwarding and no service-principal retry in `workspace-execution-resources-client.ts`, the standalone hook/editor, portable workflow action conversion, and generated `_vdab_start_databricks_job` code that uses `x-forwarded-access-token` and trusted `DATABRICKS_HOST` to POST `/api/2.2/jobs/run-now`. No focused StartDatabricksJob editor test exists. The Node aggregate validator currently accepts any generic bounded resource key although generated Python only accepts decimal job IDs, so invalid persisted values can reach runtime. Generator tests only assert that a forwarded-token helper exists somewhere in the complete workflow source and do not pin the Job request's authority/redirect/error behavior. No Databricks authorization, job listing, or run was attempted.

**Official-source finding before editing:** The current Apps authorization guide lists its supported user API scopes but omits `jobs`, although it describes Jobs as a user-authorization use case. The Databricks API scopes reference separately lists `jobs` for Jobs `List` and `Run Now`. The official Apps Lakeflow Jobs resource guide says a bound Job grants the App's service principal its selected permission (`Can view`, `Can manage run`, or `Can manage`), which does not establish per-viewer authorization and must not be substituted after a viewer denial. The Jobs privileges guide states `Run Now` executes a job as its configured Run-as identity; the triggering caller's `Can Manage Run` permission does not make the job's data access execute as that caller. Thus invocation can be authored using the viewer token, but app-issued `jobs` scope support and actual job ACL behavior remain unverified. No customer workspace is available, and the user explicitly prohibited connecting during this continuation.

**Expected paths and contract:** `databricks-app/server/src/db/project-workflow-input.ts` and `server/test/project-workflow-input.test.ts`; `server/test/project-preview-generator.test.ts`; `server/src/workspace-execution-resources-client.ts` and its tests; `xorbix-visual-databricks-app-builder.client/src/components/AppBuilderComponents/Workflow/editors/actions/StartDatabricksJobEditor.tsx` and its new test; the client `package.json` standalone lint list; plus `contracts/http-v1.md`, `workspace-binding-map.md`, `research.md`, `feature-parity-matrix.md`, `steps/05-builder-and-ai.md`, and this handoff. The portable reference remains `{ resourceType: 'databricksJob', resourceKey: <hostless job ID>, jobName }` and cannot contain external workspace or token authority.

**Acceptance criteria:** Reject non-decimal, zero, overlong, and out-of-range Databricks job IDs on save/import; keep the editor selection sourced only from the current workspace caller-token Jobs route and cover selection/error states; preserve the generated `Run Now` action using only the current viewer's forwarded token, a trusted workspace host, redirect rejection, bounded response handling, and sanitized errors; never retry with VDAB or child-App credentials. Document that Jobs execution itself follows the job's configured Run-as identity. Add focused tests, lint the new editor test, run the complete client and Node/package regressions, and parse generated Python fixtures. Do not claim Databricks consent or job execution.

**Risks:** The official Apps guide's missing `jobs` user scope conflicts with the generic Jobs API's `jobs` scope; no documentation-only conclusion can prove the Apps proxy will grant it. Per-viewer invocation authorization and project ACLs do not override the target Job's Run-as identity. The user has explicitly withheld workspace access, so live scope/permission behavior is unavailable. No job resource bound to the VDAB/child App may be used to silently bypass viewer authorization.

## Step 5 custom-module call contract — starting-state record — offline only — 2026-10-20

**Starting state reconstructed from source:** `resolveWorkflowModuleFiles` in `databricks-app/server/src/generation/preview-workflow-modules.ts` finds top-level Python function signatures and currently accepts a referenced custom function whenever its first parsed parameter is `ctx`. `preview-workflow-runtime.ts` invokes a custom workflow function with exactly one positional argument: `_VdabWorkflowContext(workflow_state)`. Therefore a module such as `def needs_extra(ctx, required):` passes preview validation but fails when the generated app invokes it. Prebuilt modules use a distinct named-argument call contract and are out of scope for this fix. The function parser recognizes only one-line `def` signatures; multiline/unsupported signatures fail as missing/incompatible. No generated customer Python is executed in the builder process.

**Exact expected paths/contracts:** `databricks-app/server/src/generation/preview-workflow-modules.ts`; `databricks-app/server/test/project-preview-generator.test.ts`; Step 5 status in `05-builder-and-ai.md` and `feature-parity-matrix.md`; this handoff. The contract is that a function selected by `CallCustomModule` must be callable by the emitted single-argument call with the VDAB context. The audit reproduced acceptance of a function requiring an additional argument. No workspace or external service was contacted.

**Acceptance criteria:** Preview rejects a referenced custom function whose Python signature requires any argument beyond `ctx`, with the existing typed `preview_workflow_module_invalid` result before emitting files. `def selected(ctx):` remains accepted. The parser's supported signature forms and fail-closed handling are documented; unrelated helper functions and `CallPrebuiltModule` named arguments retain existing behavior. Add focused regression tests and then run the full bundled regression and assembled app smoke before continuing.

**Risks:** The current parser is deliberately lightweight and is not a Python AST/security parser. Do not broaden its claims or silently change prebuilt-module argument semantics. The decision on defaulted or variadic parameters must match the emitted custom call: support them only if the validator can reliably establish that the single-argument call succeeds; otherwise reject them clearly. No new dependency, Databricks operation, or source retirement is authorized or needed.

## Step 5 custom-module call contract — completion and regression evidence — offline only — 2026-10-20

**Implementation:** `resolveWorkflowModuleFiles` now checks each `CallCustomModule` function against the actual generated invocation, which passes only the workflow context positionally. Required positional and keyword-only parameters beyond `ctx` are rejected with `preview_workflow_module_invalid`. Optional defaulted parameters and `*args`/`**kwargs` are accepted because they can be omitted. The parser now scans balanced parentheses and respects quoted strings when splitting simple one-line signatures, so inline function bodies and quoted commas work. Multiline and return-annotated signatures are rejected before source emission. `CallPrebuiltModule` still compares its named argument set against its existing parsed function parameters. ## Step 5 offline security slice — starting-state record — 2026-10-20

**Starting state reconstructed before edits:** Generated SQL and Unity Catalog Volume upload helpers attach the acting viewer's forwarded bearer token and call `urllib.request.urlopen` directly. Python's default opener follows redirects, so a redirect may forward that authorization header to a different host. Generated Jobs, ModelChat, and GenieChat already have no-redirect handlers. The packaged generated-workflow harness checks that Jobs blocks redirects but does not cover SQL or FileUpload. The package already has local SQL success/denial scenarios and the FileUpload helper source fixture; no workspace, browser, Databricks CLI/auth, Lakebase, deployment, or Marketplace access is in scope.

**Exact files/contracts planned:** `databricks-app/server/src/generation/preview-data-window-helpers.ts`, `preview-file-upload-helpers.ts`, and `preview-component-source.ts` for a shared generated no-redirect opener; `databricks-app/server/test/project-preview-generator.test.ts` for source contracts; `databricks-app/scripts/smoke-generated-workflow.py` and `generated_workflow_test_support.py` for packaged runtime success and redirect denial; every affected generated Python golden fixture; Step 5 docs, parity matrix, and this handoff. HTTP contract versions and Lakebase migrations are unchanged.

**Acceptance criteria:** Generated SQL queries and writes plus Volume uploads must use an explicit `HTTPRedirectHandler` that refuses redirects; an HTTP 302/307/308 is surfaced as a sanitized failure, and a second request to any `Location` host is never attempted. Existing SQL success and 401/403 error sanitation continue to pass. The packaged Python harness verifies normal SQL/upload traffic through the safe opener and injected redirect denials for both call paths without exposing the viewer token, redirect target, or raw response. Focused source/runtime tests and Python compilation pass, followed by a single bulk Node/client/package/smoke/PostgreSQL/.NET regression run and `git diff --check`.

**Risks:** This closes bearer-token disclosure through redirect behavior in generated SQL and Volume APIs, but does not validate workspace scopes, Databricks API authorization, SQL/Files endpoint behavior, or deployed Streamlit execution. No third-party dependency, workspace operation, Lakebase runtime completion, or legacy source retirement is planned.

## Step 5 custom-module callable parser parity — starting-state record — 2026-10-20

**Starting state reconstructed:** The client selector calls `trimStart()` and therefore offers indented/nested `def` lines as callable choices. The Node preview validator requires a column-zero single-line `def`, so those entries later fail. Both parsers reject valid multiline signatures; the Node parser also requires `:` immediately after `)`, rejecting a return annotation. Existing tests explicitly lock in acceptance of leading whitespace and rejection of multiline signatures. This is an offline source/contract mismatch; no Databricks capability is implicated.

## Lakebase Data API alternative audit — 2026-10-21

**Question:** Could the generated Lakebase slice avoid adding a Python PostgreSQL driver by using Databricks' current Lakebase Data API with Python standard-library HTTP?

**Official source review:** The Data API is documented as PostgREST-compatible table CRUD, filters, ordering, pagination, and RPC to database functions. It requires a project administrator to enable the API, expose the chosen schema, create a Postgres role for each Databricks identity, grant that role to `authenticator`, and grant the needed table/schema permissions. Selecting an existing Lakebase resource in VDAB does not itself enable or configure that API. Findings and source links are in `research.md`.

**Decision and consequence:** The Data API could cover some direct table reads/writes, but the documented interface does not provide arbitrary SQL submission. Replacing generated Lakebase `RunSql` and chart/story SQL with table CRUD or pre-created RPC functions would change the builder contract and add customer-managed functions/setup; that would not preserve the agreed feature parity. The API is therefore not used as a reduced-scope substitute. The exact `asyncpg==0.32.0` approval remains required for the proposed complete PostgreSQL query/write path. No package was added, no Data API was enabled, and no Databricks call occurred.

**Precise next action:** Wait for the already presented exact-package approval request. If approved, implement the complete generated Lakebase query/write vertical slice and exercise it with local PostgreSQL and generated-app contract tests. If declined, return with a parity-preserving alternative proposal; do not mark Lakebase paths supported or silently narrow the feature set. Continue to keep workspace calls disabled until the user supplies/authorizes a disposable workspace.
